## verify:profiles  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* KEY CLAIM 11 is wrong for two of seven types. The sizing sentence in docs/audits/receptor_sources_profiles.md section 3 hardcodes 'Li19 26, Tm32 59' MaleCNS cells while its own section-0 table (and cache/neurons.parquet) give Li19 51 and Tm32 125. 26 / 59 are Nern 2025 Sup_Table_1 RIGHT-optic-lobe counts (Li19_R 26, Tm32_R 59), i.e. one hemisphere, whereas the other five numbers in the same sentence (Tm5Y 898, TmY21 372, TmY13 432, LC11 143, Y3 627) are MaleCNS bilateral counts (Nern R-only would be 463/189/211/75/322). Two censuses are mixed in one sentence. With the doc's own median ratio (p96_per_malecns = 0.2064, printed as 0.21) the expected 96 h cluster sizes are Li19 ~11 and Tm32 ~26, not ~5 and ~12. The other five expectations (185/129/89/77/30) reproduce exactly.
* The report's gloss 'the 2 disagreements are T1 and R1-R6 (Hdc marker-silent, as in round 1)' is false for R1-R6. Recomputed from flyverse/data/expression_kurmangaliyev2020.csv: R1.6 Hdc frac_expr = 0.357 in the late pool (n=737) and 0.463 at 96h (n=514); it fails only the >=0.5 call threshold. Only T1 is marker-silent (Hdc 0.001 late, 0.004 at 96h; T1's ort is 0.763, i.e. it reads as a histamine target, not source). The doc's section-6 table itself prints 0.36, so the summary overstates it.
* KEY CLAIM 14's NT split of the unprofiled pool is off. Recomputed from the git-HEAD (round-1) receptors_by_type.csv plus cache/neurons.parquet: 532 unprofiled optic types / 30,236 cells (both confirmed), but by modal nt they are ACh 285 types / 17,836 cells (not 284 / 17,832), gaba 86 / 4,075 (confirmed), glutamate 158 / 8,305 (confirmed), histamine 1 / 7 (confirmed), serotonin 1 / 12 and unknown 1 / 1. The report's 'unknown 3 types / 17 cells' bucket lumps Mi19 (serotonin, 12 cells), T5a_unclear (unknown, 1 cell) and one 4-cell ACh-modal type (ME_LO_unclear, nt 2 Glu / 1 ACh / 1 GABA). Totals reconcile; the split is off by 1 type / 4 cells.
* Not a downloaded-file claim but a provenance-completeness one: the doc's two file tables do not account for the full GEO record. The live series listing (fetched 2026-09-11, HTTP 200) has ELEVEN supplementary files; the doc lists nine of them as downloaded (plus family.soft.gz) and names only GSE156455_matrix_early.mtx.gz under 'Not downloaded'. GSE156455_tsne_early.tsv.gz (724 KB) appears in neither table.
* Round-1 reproducibility correction NOT applied to part (B). The critic's follow-up 1 and item 6 of docs/NT_INTEGRATION.md section 7 record that builders living only in a session scratchpad are a defect ('the Davis and central builders lived only in a session scratchpad'). Part (B) of this task repeats it exactly: section 8 of docs/audits/receptor_sources_ozel2021.md (the marker-availability table, the 106-cluster candidate pool with curated markers, the 8c conclusion) was generated by scratchpad/ozel_unannotated_targets.py, which is not in scripts/ and not in the repo at all (the report's own FILES line says so). That shipped audit section is not reproducible from the repository.

### Corrections
* Fix the section-3 sizing sentence in docs/audits/receptor_sources_profiles.md (and the builder line that hardcodes it, scripts/build_kurmangaliyev2020_tables.py lines 481 and 485): use the same census for all seven types. With cache/neurons.parquet counts (Li19 51, Tm32 125) the expected 96 h cluster sizes become Li19 ~11 and Tm32 ~26 instead of ~5 and ~12; better, read the seven counts from ncell_t instead of hardcoding them so the paragraph cannot drift from the section-0 table.
* Restate the R1-R6 disagreement: it is not 'Hdc marker-silent'. R1.6 Hdc frac_expr is 0.357 (72-96 h) and 0.463 (96 h); the cluster misses the >=0.5 call threshold, it is not silent. Only T1 is silent (0.001 / 0.004). Quote both numbers wherever the two disagreements are summarised.
* Correct the part-B pool split in docs/audits/receptor_sources_ozel2021.md section 8b to ACh 285 types / 17,836 cells, Glu 158 / 8,305, GABA 86 / 4,075, His 1 / 7, 5-HT 1 / 12 (Mi19), unknown 1 / 1 (T5a_unclear), and state the rule used for mixed-label types (ME_LO_unclear, 4 cells, 2 Glu / 1 ACh / 1 GABA, is the type that moved the ACh count).
* Add GSE156455_tsne_early.tsv.gz (724 KB) to the 'Not downloaded' table so the 11 GEO supplementary files are fully accounted for.
* Move scratchpad/ozel_unannotated_targets.py into the repo (e.g. scripts/search_ozel_unannotated_targets.py) and cite it in the header of section 8 of receptor_sources_ozel2021.md; as shipped, that section cannot be regenerated from the repository, which is the exact defect round 1 was told to stop repeating.
* Pin or record the mutable inputs of the audit document. scripts/build_kurmangaliyev2020_tables.py reads flyverse/data/receptors_by_type.csv (for section 5a) and cache/ (for every synapse number), and both changed after the tables were written: cache/ was rebuilt at 19:53 with connectome.TYPE_NT_OVERRIDE and receptors_by_type.csv at 20:06 by the round-2 derive task (it now contains kurmangaliyev2020-derived rows). Measured consequences: (i) rerunning the builder unchanged today reproduces both CSVs byte-identically but REWRITES the audit - section 5a becomes self-referential (round-1-alone block turns into exact 59 types / 30,041 unprofiled cells and the 'types whose tier improves' table disappears entirely); (ii) with the new cache the optic totals move from 49,414,461 to 49,441,359 input synapses (+0.054%) and 51,923,266 to 51,956,714 output, Tm5Y in-syn 406,046 -> 406,050, Y3 805,508 -> 805,609, the 8-type gain 3,133,488 -> 3,133,700. Add a line to section 5a naming the receptors_by_type.csv revision used (git HEAD c0332e3) and the cache build date, or take the round-1 table from git instead of the working tree.
* Soften the phrasing 'the four libraries titled W1118_Adult_* ... are the 96h cells of the metadata': they are 13,237 of the 22,659 96 h cells; the other 9,422 come from the DGRP multiplexed pool. The claim that follows (no post-eclosion timepoint) is unaffected.
* Remove or use FRAC_ON = 0.2 in scripts/build_kurmangaliyev2020_tables.py line 65: its comment says it is 'the on call used in the audit document's transmitter cross-check', but the cross-check hardcodes >= 0.5 and the constant is never referenced.
* Optional precision: the 'Nern 2025 SI catalogue has 0 occurrences of marker / gene / express*' statement is true of MOESM1 only. The same ESM set's MOESM3 (Peer Review File) has 21 'marker' and 46 'gene' occurrences - all referee discussion, none a type-level marker list, so the conclusion is unchanged, but the claim should name the file. Also pdfinfo reports 72 pages, while '70-page' is the authors' own wording for the summary inside it.

### Confirmed
* Central negative claim CONFIRMED. GSE156455_metadata_main.tsv.gz re-read independently: 193,727 cells, columns barcode/set/rep/trep/genotype/time/class/type/subtype, barcode set identical to the barcodes file. type==Tm5Y|TmY21|TmY13|LC11|Y3|Li19|Tm32 -> 0 cells, subtype -> 0 cells, and no case-insensitive or substring variant exists among the 199 labels (only unrelated TmY3 / Tm3). 187 type labels, 196 subtype labels, 199 clusters, 61 non-numbered labels + C2 + C3 = 63 annotated names, 136 unannotated (104 N## with 66,284 cells, 19 G##, 12 C##, 1 R##). Time counts 24h 32,382 / 36h 19,993 / 48h 67,447 / 60h 11,935 / 72h 30,071 / 84h 9,240 / 96h 22,659 and class counts N 162,709 / G 15,689 / C 11,354 / R 3,975 all match to the cell.
* Files and hashes CONFIRMED: all 10 files in data/external/kurmangaliyev2020/ recomputed (sha256sum + stat) and every byte size and digest matches the section-2 table and the flyverse/data/manifest.json external.kurmangaliyev2020 entry; the repo's own checker, PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8 python scripts/fetch_data.py --verify, prints 'ok' for all 10. Live GEO FTP listing confirms matrix_early 152M and metadata_main last modified 2020-11-04 (the T4c<->T4d-corrected release the doc claims to use), and that the series ships only MEX matrices + barcodes/features/metadata/tsne/README, i.e. no per-cluster summary table.
* Matrix streaming CONFIRMED by an independent pass. I re-streamed GSE156455_matrix_main.mtx.gz with my own code (genes located by FBgn, different chunk size, separate accumulator): header dims 17,561 x 193,727, 331,223,007 non-zeros, exactly 331,223,007 data lines read, max gene index 17,561, max cell index 193,727, median total UMI per cell 5,187. Versus the builder's cache _percell_54genes.npz: totals max abs diff 0.0, and per-cell counts for ChAT, VAChT, VGlut, Gad1, Hdc, GluClalpha, ort, Nmdar2, lncRNA:roX1, lncRNA:roX2 all max abs diff 0.
* Derived tables CONFIRMED reproducible. I copied scripts/build_kurmangaliyev2020_tables.py to a scratchpad, redirected only its output paths, and reran it: flyverse/data/type_map_kurmangaliyev2020.csv and flyverse/data/expression_kurmangaliyev2020.csv came out byte-identical to the shipped files (diff empty). Structure confirmed: type_map 221 rows / 199 clusters / 85 (cluster,type) pairs / 69 MaleCNS types / 136 unmatched; expression 3,536 rows x 60 columns, 0 NaN, 199 clusters, 54 gene columns, time labels 24h..96h + late + all, both metrics 1,768 rows each (199*9*2 - 3,536 = 46 rows absent = 23 empty cluster/time combos).
* End-to-end value check CONFIRMED. From MY OWN streamed per-cell counts I recomputed frac_expr and mean_log1p_cp10k for 10 (cluster, time) combinations spanning exact, alias, class, parent-pool and unannotated clusters (T1 late, Mi1 96h, R1.6 late, R7.8 late, N54 late, Tm9 all, Dm3 late, T4.T5 96h, L1 72h, T5d late): n_cells identical in every case, max |frac diff| <= 1.1e-16, max |mean diff| <= 2.6e-07 (float32). The aggregation, the late/all pooling and the parent-pool handling are correct.
* Gene panel CONFIRMED: all 54 plan symbols are present verbatim in the r6.29 feature list (17,561 genes, 1 NaN symbol row FBgn0036414 irrelevant to the panel); ChAT=FBgn0000303, KaiR1D=FBgn0038837 (CG3822 - the correct gene, not Davis's CG8916 error), mAChR-C=FBgn0029909, Octalpha2R=FBgn0038653, lncRNA:roX1/2 present. No translation needed, unlike Ozel's 53/54.
* Tier assignment CONFIRMED and tier-inflation test PASSED. Counts reproduce (exact 51 clusters / 51 types, alias 2/2 R1.6->R1-R6 + Lpi3.4->LPi34, fuzzy 2/2 Tm29 + Pm3, class 8/23, 69 distinct types after the Tm9 and T4/T5 overlaps, 136 unmatched). I then ran the round-1 Pm1 test against Nern 2025 Sup_Table_7 for every mapped label: all 51 exact-tier labels are rows flagged 'matched as = 1-to-1', and the only name that spans two OL types is Schlegel Dm11 (-> Dm11 + Dm-DRA2), which the builder already flags in EXACT_NOTES. The round-1 corrections that apply here were applied: Tm29 demoted to fuzzy with the name-collision evidence, Pm3 fuzzy (CB3856 / Pm09), Dm11 split noted. All 69 mapped types are optic (ol_intrinsic 51, ol_sensory 10, visual_projection 8), so 'central brain / VNC 0 by construction' holds.
* Coverage and union CONFIRMED (against the cache state the agent used). The rerun reproduces the section-5 tables exactly except for a cache drift noted below: exact 51 types / 58,823 cells / 18,227,376 in-syn (56.2% / 36.9%), alias 2 / 3,496, fuzzy 2 / 613, class 14 / 6,946, unmatched 559 / 34,811 / 29,434,360 (33.3% / 59.6%); visual_projection exact 8 types / 1,448 cells (15.7%). With the round-1 receptors_by_type.csv taken from git HEAD (commit c0332e3), section 5a reproduces too: 0 MaleCNS types get a first profile, unprofiled optic cells unchanged at 30,273 (28.9%), unprofiled input synapses unchanged at 24,852,260 (50.3%), and exactly 8 types (T4a-d, T5a-d; 13,580 cells) improve fuzzy -> exact.
* Transmitter cross-check CONFIRMED: 85 (cluster,type) pairs, 83 agree, disagreements T1 and R1-R6, reproduced row-for-row by the rerun.
* Sex check CONFIRMED and strengthened: 483 of 193,727 cells (0.2493%) reach 5 roX cp10k, 97.86% have zero roX UMIs, the highest library is W1118_24h_A_4 at 1.59%. The whole atlas contains 4,142 roX1 and 4,261 roX2 UMIs in 193,727 cells - a male pool would carry orders of magnitude more. Female verdict is solid.
* Stage claim CONFIRMED from the SOFT record: 36 samples (GSM4731869-4731904), four titled W1118_Adult_A_1..4 and exactly four samples carrying 'age: 96h APF' (others 24h x4, 48h x8, 72h x4, 0-96h x16 DGRP), overall design 'covers 9 stages of pupal development (every 12 hours)', Genome_build FlyBase 6.29, PubMed 33125872. All 13,237 cells of the four Adult libraries carry time '96h' in the metadata. No post-eclosion timepoint exists.
* Paywall claim CONFIRMED: Europe PMC returns PMID 33125872 with no PMCID and isOpenAccess=N; https://www.cell.com/neuron/fulltext/S0896-6273(20)30793-6 returns HTTP 403. I additionally searched Europe PMC for a free preprint (SRC:PPR, title and author queries) and found none for this paper, so there is indeed no accessible version in which to read the annotation method.
* Part (B) marker claims CONFIRMED. pdftotext -layout on data/external/nern2025/nature_esm/41586_2025_8746_MOESM1_ESM.pdf gives 4,231 lines with a real text layer (LC11 appears 34x, Tm5Y 71x, Y3 163x) and 0 case-insensitive occurrences of marker / gene / express. Sup_Table_6 rows match the report exactly: Tm5Y SS03722 (VT031495 x R94H07) + SS03753 (R41G07 x VT031495); TmY13 SS12598 (R56B05 x VT043927); LC11 OL0015B (Wu 2016), OL0031B (a multi-type line 'LC16,LC11,LC13,(LC9),(LC10),(LC6),(LLPC)') and OL0043B; Y3 SS02577 + SS02605; Li19 SS24377 + SS90337; Tm32 SS02439; TmY21 none. Davis elife-50901-supp1-v2.xlsx: A_all_drivers 129 rows with 0 drivers for any of the seven (the sole LC11 string is 'a few LC11 cells' in another driver's contamination note; the Y3 hit is TmY3), D_markerGenes 262 rows over exactly pr 108 / muscle 76 / glia 60 / pigment 18.
* Part (B) Ozel numbers CONFIRMED: type_map_ozel2021.csv has 136 unmatched clusters of which 106 carry the 'unannotated neuronal cluster' evidence, and the mixture-model transmitter calls over those 106 are ACh 45, Glu 33, GABA 25, ACh+Glu 1, GABA+Glu 1, ACh+GABA 1 - exactly as claimed. 532 unprofiled optic types / 30,236 cells also reproduce (the 30,273 in the profiles doc is the same set plus 37 untyped optic cells).
* Model-facing conclusion CONFIRMED on CPU (no GPU work was needed and none should have been run): none of Tm5Y, TmY21, TmY13, LC11, Y3, Li19, Tm32 appears in receptors_by_type.csv either at git HEAD (round 1) or in the current round-2 rebuild, so their input stays on NT_SIGN. Their glutamatergic input recomputed from cache/: Tm5Y 102,017 of 406,050 (25.1%), TmY21 60,677 / 201,184 (30.2%), TmY13 73,179 / 241,195 (30.3%), Y3 198,878 / 805,609 (24.7%), LC11 42,630 / 342,941 (12.4%), Li19 12,687 / 62,505, Tm32 23,594 / 169,622 - matching the report's 102k / 61k / 73k / 199k.

## verify:score:rules  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* KEY CLAIM 8 / doc R2.5 'Pinned loom (out/loom2_<mode>_s{0,1,2}.txt, deterministic: 3 identical seeds per mode)' and 'the three seeds of a mode are identical to the spike in every reported value' -- FALSE for off and class. Diffing the shipped files: off s0 vs s1==s2 differ in spikes/step (t=0.5/0.6/0.7/0.8 s: 27/172/89/7 vs 25/19/83/4), MDN (10 vs 0) and LC16 (1 vs 0) at t=0.6 s; class s0 vs s1==s2 differ in the GF trace itself (0 0 0 5 5 14 26 15 6 vs 0 0 0 5 5 13 24 12 4), TTMn (0 0 0 0 10 8 6 14 5 vs 0 0 0 0 7 2 7 5 13) and spikes/step (33 23 14 1 vs 20 18 11 26). Only abs is identical across its three seeds. Worse for the replicate claim: in EVERY mode loom2_*_s1.txt and loom2_*_s2.txt are byte-identical, so the 'three seeds' are at most two distinct samples (one for abs). The probe IS deterministic per seed across jobs and nodes -- my cluster rerun (run sk2-rules-e78638, 6 jobs / 0 failed) reproduced out/loom2_off_s0.txt and out/loom2_abs_s0.txt byte-for-byte on the probe lines, and a fresh seed 5 (out/skeptic2/loom_abs_s5.txt) is byte-identical to abs s0 -- so the defensible statement is 'reproducible per seed; the seed barely decorrelates', not '3 identical seeds' presented as 3/3 replication of the abs escape.
* KEY CLAIM 3, second half: 'The classfb flips are glutamate onto unprofiled GABAergic (3,516,908 syn) and glutamatergic (3,637,340 syn) cells.' Those two totals are ALL classfb flips grouped by postsynaptic transmitter, profiled targets included, not the unprofiled/fallback ones. Recomputed from cache/ with connectome.receptor_signs(net_rule='class', nt_class_fallback=True): flips at tier nt_class (i.e. actually decided by the class baseline) are gaba 3,216,485 syn / 594,317 entries and glutamate 3,497,828 syn / 820,302 entries = 6,714,313 syn / 1,414,619 entries; the other 439,935 syn (gaba 300,423 + glutamate 139,512) are ordinary class-rule flips onto profiled GABA/Glu targets that classfb inherits. The doc's R2.6 sentence is also internally inconsistent: it says 7.59 M synapses flip 'onto every unprofiled GABAergic / glutamatergic cell in the CNS ... (3.52 M / 3.64 M syn)', but 3.52 + 3.64 = 7.16 M, and the missing 0.44 M land on cholinergic (320,203), histaminergic (62,361), octopaminergic (48,398), dopaminergic (2,551), serotonergic (4,696) and unknown (1,315) targets.
* Doc R2.7 caveat 'scripts/probe_bitter.py gained --seed and --receptor-nt-class-fallback, scripts/probe_figure_ground.py gained --seed; no other script changed' -- FALSE, and scripts/benchmark.py is absent from the report's FILES list. `git show c0332e3:scripts/benchmark.py | grep -c nt_class_fallback` = 0, while the working-tree benchmark.py carries `--receptor-nt-class-fallback` (line 767) and `--cache-dir` (line ~97) and sets `p.receptor_nt_class_fallback` (line 179); both flags are required by the classfb run and by every rm2_* run (config.cache_dir = /mnt/beegfs/neurome/runs/ntov-r2-e7706e/cache_override in all ten JSONs). Whoever authored those hunks (the diff interleaves a concurrent slow-term task's --slow-* flags), the shipped caveat as written is wrong.

### Corrections
* loom.GF_peak_hz (the legacy in-suite loom) is filed under 'the chaotic sections ... Differences of that size between modes mean nothing', but under abs it is not scatter: 28.04 / 27.05 / 27.05 (their three runs) plus 28.04 (my abs_r4) against off 43.88 / 36.94 / 36.74 plus 38.00 (my off_r4) -- four-vs-four with no overlap and a 1 Hz spread inside abs against 7 Hz inside off. It is a systematic ~10 Hz LOSS on that check (still PASS, criterion >= 20) and sits awkwardly beside the verdict sentence 'no check worsens' and the framing that abs strengthens the loom response. It should be reported as a measured cost of abs, with the note that the legacy loom and the demo loom_escape move in opposite directions under the same rule.
* Update the off-escape denominator: with my fourth off run the demo loom escapes in 3 of 12 seeds (peaks 21.5-36.0 Hz), not 3 of 9, and off scores 26/1/2 in 2 of 4 suite runs and 24/3/2 in the other 2 (the doc says '26/1/2, 24/3/2, 26/1/2'). abs is 12/12 seeds at 47-74 Hz over four runs. The direction is strengthened; the counts in the verdict and in R2.2/R2.6 are now stale.
* 'LC10a per-cell max drive' / 'best cell 0.12-0.17 mV' is the maximum POSITIVE (apple - none) drive, not the largest |drive|: in class s0/s1 the largest-magnitude LC10a cells are -0.26 and -0.29 mV and in off s1 they are -0.18 mV (out/fg2_*_s*.txt 'top cells' lists). The 7 mV-threshold conclusion is unaffected, but the column should be labelled as the positive max.
* 'TmY21 unchanged' (SUMMARY and the R2.4 'reads' column) overstates what the table shows: z goes -0.45/-0.39 (off) -> -0.71/-0.75 (class) -> -0.44/-1.20 (abs) -> +0.01/-0.29 (nonmda). What is actually unchanged is TmY21's input (0 % matched, 0 flipped); say that instead.
* R2.4 says Mi1's '13,013 R8 histamine synapses are silenced'. 13,013 is the total silenced input to Mi1; the R8* share is 12,491 (the figure KEY CLAIM 12 gives), the remaining 522 being R7y/R7p/R7d/R7_unclear/R7R8_unclear 328 and T1 194. Both numbers are right; the R2.4 wording attributes all of them to R8.
* The mechanism attributed to abs's +18 Hz loom gain ('Tm9's flipped input (Dm12 12,144 + Mi13 5,037 syn) raises |T5a| 0.05 -> 0.13 and LPLC2 3 -> 5') is inferred, not tested. abs also flips T1 (62,361 syn, 2.7x Tm9's 22,946) and Dm9 (25,467) in the same medulla, and no run exists with the Tm9 row held at -1. Since the doc itself flags the Tm9 row as the one abs flip resting on Davis 2020 alone against Kurmangaliyev / FCA / Davie at -1 (confirmed: Tm9 KaiR1D 107.32 TPM vs GluClalpha 0.00, alt_sources all fast=-1), that control is the decisive missing experiment before step 8 adopts abs.
* The 9 pinned-loom jobs print no device line (unlike probe_bitter's 'device: cuda; cuda available: True' and probe_figure_ground's 'device cuda'), so 'every job asserted torch.cuda.is_available()' is not checkable from out/loom2_*.txt; what those files do prove is the cache and rule (they print 'fast sign changed on 63,380 / 257,578 entries', which are the override-cache values -- the pre-override cache gives 63,251 / 257,021).
* '17 jobs' for 37 output files means several commands per job (5 benchmark + 5 bitter x3 seeds + 4 fg x2 + 3 loom x3 = 17), i.e. the per-mode probe seeds are sequential commands inside one job rather than independent jobs. The arithmetic is consistent and worth stating, since it bears on how independent the 'replicates' are.
* Denominator nit: the glutamate |W| total is 21,205,507 by float64 summation of |W.data| (the report and the doc both write 21,205,508); the 4.15 / 0.60 / 8.05 / 35.81 % shares are unaffected.

### Confirmed
* KEY CLAIM 1 + the COVERAGE block reproduce EXACTLY from an independent run of connectome.receptor_signs on cache/ (167,106 neurons, nnz 25,578,600, sum|W| 121,460,584, W content md5 ef23cc27bea13be7f6a96f3c04fd3737 = the value they quote): matched 7,654,565 edges (0.299257) / 31,627,236 |W| (0.260391); exact 5,074,564 / 20,820,728 (0.198391 / 0.171420); fuzzy 1,463,238 / 5,714,363; alias 427,490 / 1,446,049; class 689,273 / 3,646,096; fallback 17,669,591 / 89,833,348 (0.690796 / 0.739609); pre_unknown 254,444 / 0. classfb: nt_class 17,317,086 / 86,702,413 (0.677015 / 0.713832), matched 24,971,651 / 118,329,649 (0.976271 / 0.974223), fallback 352,505 / 3,130,935. Per-module matched fractions optic 0.5312 / VP 0.3761 / MB 0.9317 / AL 0.4779 / central 0.0475 / descending 0.0174 / mechanosensory 0.0324 / gustatory 0.0011 / VNC 0, and classfb 0.9994 / 0.9979 / 1.0000 / 0.9905 / 0.9838 / 0.9901 / 0.9974 / 0.4890 / 0.9088 -- all match.
* KEY CLAIM 2 reproduces exactly on both caches. Adopted cache: class 257,578 entries / 962,932 syn changed, flips 240,199 / 879,459 (4.15 % of 21,205,507 glutamate syn), silenced 17,379 / 83,473 histamine; abs 63,380 / 210,935, flips 46,001 / 127,462 (0.60 %); nonmda 389,284 / 1,789,469, flips 371,905 / 1,705,996 (8.05 %); classfb 1,672,197 / 7,677,245, flips 1,654,818 / 7,593,772 (35.81 %); 0 flips +1->-1 in every rule; silenced identical in all four. On out/cache_pre_override (W md5 289047c3e6cf921a9745f1e0c292fb4d, glutamate cells 29,616): 878,406 / 127,309 / 1,703,719 -- the task's quoted numbers, exactly, and the override adds 91 glutamate cells (29,616 -> 29,707, unknown 2,467 -> 2,361) = TmY14, as claimed.
* KEY CLAIM 3, table part: receptors_by_type.csv selector rows are exactly as quoted -- <nt=gaba> glutamate fast_net +1 / fast_net_abs -1, Nmdar2 lead, fast_pos_val 752.66 vs fast_neg_val 1800.25; <nt=glutamate> glutamate +1 / abs -1, 738.37 vs 1918.82; <nt=acetylcholine> glutamate -1 in class, abs and nonmda. I also verified the consequence the report draws: connectome.receptor_signs(net_rule='abs', nt_class_fallback=True).fast_sign is element-identical to the no-fallback abs lookup on all 25,578,600 entries (0 differing), i.e. abs+fallback really is a no-op on the fast sign (it does change slow_sign on 17,114,897 entries and the tier on 17,317,086, which the report correctly restricts its claim away from).
* KEY CLAIMS 4, 5, 9, 10 verified against the ten shipped JSONs (out/rm2_{off,class,abs,nonmda,classfb}.json, rm2_{off,abs}_r{2,3}.json, rm_ntov.json). Totals: off 26/1/2, class 25/2/2, abs 27/0/2, nonmda 24/3/2, classfb 21/6/2, off_r2 24/3/2, off_r3 26/1/2, abs_r2 27/0/2, abs_r3 27/0/2. Every one of the 29 measured values and statuses in the R2.2 table matches the JSON, including bitter.shiu_sugar 123.54 P -> 6.63 F (class), walk.power_max 73.18 F -> 46.10 P (abs), walk_gf.p99 51.63 F and rotation.group -2.32 F (nonmda), and the six classfb failures. All ten runs record cache_dir = /mnt/beegfs/neurome/runs/ntov-r2-e7706e/cache_override, nt_counts glutamate 29,707, neurons 167,106, device NVIDIA B200, seeds [0,1,2], backend 'native (cuda_kernels, cuda_graphs, event_driven, warp)', and fast_sign_changed_entries 257,578 / 63,380 / 389,284 / 1,672,197. Every per-seed loom_escape triple and walk_gf p99 in KEY CLAIM 9 and every rotation flip_hz in KEY CLAIM 10 read back exactly.
* The determinism argument is sound: rm2_off is bit-identical to the adoption reference rm_ntov on the Brain-only sections (taste 5.845468997955322, smell PN 11.18769645690918 / KC 1426, walk.power_max 73.18273162841797, bitter.shiu 123.53939666748047, calibrated 4.565924626588822) and differs from the pre-override rm_off (79.06 / 12.39 / 1599), so the concurrent tasks' brain.py/optic.py/fly.py edits shipped with the batch did not move the off path. abs's 46.10 / 10.93 / 137.42 / 7.86 / 816 are identical in rm2_abs, _r2, _r3.

## verify:score:object  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* VERDICT ROBUSTNESS (key claims 1-2, SUMMARY 'FAIL in all 10 runs' / 'no cell crosses 1 Hz only with the ball'): refuted as a statement about the model. I reran the IDENTICAL configuration (shipped scripts/probe_object_sweep.py, --receptor-model off --seed 0, same override cache /mnt/beegfs/neurome/runs/ntov-r2-e7706e/cache_override, B200, torch 2.11.0+cu128) five times; one of them PASSES. D:\Projects\flyverse\out\skobj\sk_off_s0.json: verdict {'LC10a': {'rate_pass': true}, 'pass': true} -- LC10a max cell 1.167 Hz with the ball and cells_over_1hz 1, vs 0.833 Hz and 0 cells without. Tally over all 17 runs of the exact protocol that now exist (their 10 + my 7: cluster batches sk-obj2-e2860c and sk-obj3-97a62e): 16 FAIL, 1 PASS; over the 5 off/seed-0 runs alone 4 FAIL, 1 PASS. The rate quantum is 1/12 s = 0.0833 Hz, so the '1 Hz' clause is decided by 12 vs 13 spikes in the 12 s window. The report's own section 6.2 says the criterion cannot discriminate, but its section 7 verdict ('FAIL, all 10 runs, every mode') and key claim 2 read as a stable negative; the outcome is a coin flip at the spontaneous-rate ceiling.
* NOISE-FLOOR NUMBERS (key claim 5): 'With no stimulus 0.1-0.4 % of LC11 cell-frames exceed +7 mV (per-frame minima -7.6 to -11.3 mV)'. Recomputed per run from the ten shipped JSONs (field drive_frames_over_7mv_frac / drive_min_mv): the none-condition fraction is 0.012-0.404 %, not 0.1-0.4 % -- obj_rect_off_s1.json 0.012 %, obj_rect_off_s0.json 0.037 % are 3-10x below the stated floor (ball condition 0.065-0.425 %). And the quoted minima are the BALL condition's: none runs give -8.49 to -11.49 mV (-11.49 in obj_class_s0.json), while -7.56 mV is the ball run of obj_rect_class_s0.json. Correct statement over the 10 runs and both conditions: 0.012-0.425 % of LC11 cell-frames > +7 mV, per-frame minima -7.56 to -11.49 mV.
* SWEEP-LOCKED TUNING RATIOS (key claim 3) are a two-run estimate that does not survive replication, so the mode ordering it supports is not real. Reported LC11 ball/none ratio 1.36 for sign-class vs 1.06 off / 1.05 abs. Adding my replicates of the same configuration (out/skobj/sk_class_s0.json, out/skobj/skobj/sk_class_s0_r2.json, _r3.json): pooled over 5 sign-class runs the ratio is 1.16; off pooled over 6 runs is 1.10 (was 1.06). Per-run LC11 tuning ball/none under sign-class: 11.04/8.56, 8.22/5.56, 9.20/11.00, 9.51/6.28, 7.31/7.76 -- the no-ball control exceeds the ball in 2 of 5. The claim that sign-class raises the sweep-locked tuning more than off/abs is inside the run-to-run scatter.
* PER-MODE DIFFERENCE MEANS (key claim 4) are quoted as single two-seed means without their scatter. LC11 diff_best_cell_mean_mv under sign-class: reported +0.23; the five runs I now have give +0.304, +0.150, +0.215, +0.481, +0.197 (mean +0.27, range +0.15..+0.48). Under off: reported +0.04; six runs give +0.050, +0.034, +0.084, +0.080, +0.131, +0.074 (mean +0.076, range +0.034..+0.131). The off-vs-class separation survives, but every quoted value has ~100 % run-to-run scatter and two seeds do not measure it.

### Corrections
* 'diff_best_cell_mean_mv', reported as 'best cell's (ball - none) time-mean', is float((m - other_mean).max()) (probe_object_sweep.py line 238) -- the MAXIMUM over cells of the per-cell difference, not the difference at the best-driven cell. It is the more generous statistic, so it does not weaken a FAIL, but the label should read 'max over cells of the (ball - none) time-mean drive'.
* The JSON fields diff_peak_mv and diff_peak_cells_over_7mv (printed in every run's table, e.g. '+14.71' and 118 of 143 LC11 cells under off) are a frame-by-frame subtraction of two INDEPENDENT stochastic runs and are pure noise; they should be dropped or flagged, since a reader of out/obj/*_s?.txt sees '+0.05/+14.71/+3.09 mV' in the 'diff best mean/peak/tuning' column.
* smooth_peak (line 210) computes (cs[w:] - cs[:-w]) / w without a leading zero, so the reported 'peak 100 ms' boxcar starts at frame 1 and misses the first 100 ms window. Immaterial to the conclusions.
* Key claim 6 and section 6.4's 'LC11 spontaneous rate 0.006 -> 0.000 Hz (off)' quotes the BALL condition's population mean rate (0.0055 -> 0.0003); the no-stimulus values are 0.0015 -> 0.0000 Hz. Same mislabel as in claim 5.
* Key claim 7's 'sign-class raises LC11 time-mean drive +0.08 -> +0.22 mV ... in both ball and no-ball conditions': the no-ball value is +0.156 mV, not +0.22 (ball +0.084 -> +0.221). Over my 4-5 replicates of seed 0: off ball +0.096 / none +0.087; sign-class ball +0.235 / none +0.171. The point (the rise is condition-independent) stands; the number does not.
* Key claim 12's LPLC2 range '+0.20 to +0.45 mV' is the per-mode seed-mean; the per-run range over the 10 runs is +0.126 to +0.589 mV. The report's inference that the NOTES-9 LPLC2 result was a stimulus-geometry difference is now demonstrable rather than conjectural: LPLC2's (ball - none) best-cell drive scales +0.11 / +0.97 / +1.37 / +3.67 mV with 11 / 22 / 28 / 43 deg objects (out/skobj/sk_static_off_s0.json, sk_r010_off_s0.json, sk_near_off_s0.json, sk_r020_off_s0.json) -- I suggest adding that ladder to docs/audits/object_sweep.md section 6.3 in place of 'probably held the ball higher'.
* docs/audits/object_sweep.md section 7 should state the replication result: the FAIL verdict is stochastic. 1 of 17 runs of the identical protocol passes on LC10a's spontaneous rate crossing 1 Hz; the per-run pass/fail carries no information and only the (ball - none) magnitudes (0.03-0.48 mV against 7 mV) do. Section 6.2 already argues this; the verdict section contradicts it by counting 10/10.
* Two seeds per mode is not enough for any ratio quoted in section 3 or 6.1. The cheapest fix is 5+ repeats of one mode (they cost ~2.5 min each on one GPU-share; my 8-job batch took 3.1 min wall) or a none-vs-none run pair per mode to give the difference statistics an explicit null.
* My verification artefacts (new files, nothing of theirs edited): D:\Projects\flyverse\scripts\skeptic_object_check.py (geometry-overriding wrapper that imports the probe unmodified), D:\Projects\flyverse\scripts\skeptic_object_env.py (script-provenance and cache check), and the fetched runs D:\Projects\flyverse\out\skobj\{env.txt, sk_off_s0, sk_class_s0, sk_static_off_s0, sk_near_off_s0, sk_r010_off_s0, sk_r020_off_s0, sk_r020_class_s0}.{json,txt} plus D:\Projects\flyverse\out\skobj\skobj\{sharedcache.txt, sk_off_s0_r{2,3,4}, sk_class_s0_r{2,3}}.{json,txt} (cluster batches sk-obj2-e2860c and sk-obj3-97a62e, 14 jobs, 1 failed on a missing out/ directory in the first attempt, all others device cuda / NVIDIA B200).

## verify:score:compass  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* KEY CLAIM 8 is superseded, not true as a description of the work product: the five jobs of batch cx-glno-f2e987 did NOT stay running. API status now: 6bc1ffcb22eb / 887af4b0fcf4 / 40283e8ea3cc / 767c548e8e79 / cf9577d340c4 all 'completed' exit 0, started 2026-09-12T03:18:03Z and finished 03:21:27-03:22:32Z, i.e. 3.4-4.5 minutes after submission. All 30 runs and their logs were sitting in /mnt/beegfs/neurome/runs/cx-glno-f2e987/out/ (cx_glno_{base,glu,ach,sign-class,sign-abs}.{json,txt}, 6 rows each) while the report said 'no simulation numbers exist yet'. I fetched them and ran the report's own scripts/cx_glno.py --report on them without modification; it works and produces the full table.
* docs/audits/cx_glno.md section 2, '(the model-wide 878,406 / 127,309 flipped synapses)': wrong connectome. Those two numbers are the values for out/cache_pre_override (no TYPE_NT_OVERRIDE): class 239,642 entries / 878,406 syn, abs 45,872 / 127,309 -- and 127,309 is verbatim the round-1 figure in receptor_verification.md (verify:score). On the adopted TYPE_NT_OVERRIDE cache that every one of the five jobs actually compiled and ran, the flips are class 240,199 entries / 879,459 syn and abs 46,001 / 127,462 (identical on cache/ and out/cache_override). The sentence's whole point is to say what changes outside the ring in the runs, so it must quote the override numbers.
* docs/audits/cx_glno.md section 3, 'an inhibitory GLNO at gE 2 removes about 40 % of a PEN's EPG-volley drive': factor-2 error, wrong gain. GLNO->PEN is not touched by type_path_gain; EPG->PEN is multiplied by gE. Recomputed with brain._shaped_weights under the run's own gains: at gE 2 the EPG volley delivers +159.8 mV per PEN (mean; +10.11 mV per pair), so GLNO's 33.0 mV is 20.6 %; at gE 1.75 it is 23.6 %. The ~41 % figure (33.0 / 79.9) is the gain-x1 number. The section-1 table's +5.05 / +79.9 mV EPG->PEN entries are likewise x1 values printed next to a protocol that runs gE 2 and 1.75, with no gain stated.
* Round-1 correction NOT applied (receptor_verification.md, follow-ups item 4 COMPASS (2)): 'Record ... that the ExR4/ExR6/ExR5/Delta7 -> EPG glutamate (19,000 syn, 9.8 % of EPG input) is fast GluCl inhibition by the E-PG driver data'. No such statement, and no ExR->EPG synapse count, appears anywhere in docs/audits/cx_glno.md (grep for GluCl / ExR5 / ExR4 / 9.8 returns nothing but the abs changed_by_pre_type list) nor in docs/audits/receptor_integration.md. Only the second half of that item -- 'PEN's only profile is the Mi1-contaminated Davis PB_3' -- is recorded. For the record, my recount: glutamate onto EPG 19,110 raw syn = 9.49 % of EPG's 201,371 raw input (ExR6 6,236, ExR5 6,057, Delta7 4,294, ExR4 1,660 = 18,247 = 9.06 %), so the round-1 '9.8 %' is itself 0.3 pp high.
* Latent defect in the shipped scripts/cx_wedge.py: --out defaults to docs/audits/ and structure() now receives the override connectome (line 653, 'res, cells, c = structure(out_dir, c=c)'). Running the invocation the round-1 follow-up and cx_glno.py's own docstring quote -- 'python scripts/cx_wedge.py --sim ... --nt-override GLNO=glutamate' without --no-structure -- silently overwrites docs/audits/cx_wedge.json, cx_wedge_matrices.npz and the 15 cx_wedge_*.png with numbers from a GLNO=glutamate brain. Not triggered by cx_glno.py (which calls simulate() directly) and the tracked files are still clean in git, but the flag is user-facing and unguarded.

### Corrections
* The two answers the report left blank, read from their own outputs (30 runs, /mnt/beegfs/neurome/runs/cx-glno-f2e987/out/) plus my fresh-seed batch (18 runs, /mnt/beegfs/neurome/runs/sk-glno-55aaf4/out/, seeds 3,4,5). ANSWER 1 -- the receptor model does not touch the compass, dynamically as well as structurally: sign-class is BIT-IDENTICAL to base on every ring metric at both gains and all three seeds (pre / 0.5 / 2 / 5 s in and out means, in_above, out_above, vs, centre, PEN, Delta7, GLNO, PEG, ER/ExR), including the seed-1 gE-1.75 collapse to 42 Hz. The only field that moves is the rest-of-brain rate, 0.033 / 0.034 / 0.033 -> 0.039 / 0.040 / 0.040 Hz, which proves the receptor stage was actually active. sign-abs moves the ring a few Hz (the 1,487 ER/ExR abs flips feeding EPG/PEN/PEG at gR 1): 5 s bump 200.7 / 199.7 / 203.0 vs base 201.0 / 201.4 / 204.3 at gE 2, and 162.9 / 161.4 / 165.0 vs 164.0 / 42.0 / 169.3 at gE 1.75 -- inside seed scatter.
* ANSWER 2 -- the silent PEN <-> GLNO loop does matter, and the sign decides the persistence window. EPG in-rate at 5 s per seed (0,1,2 theirs | 3,4,5 mine): gE 2.0 base 201/201/204 | 196/192/201, glu 180/181/184 | 179/176/182, ach 234/233/230 | 229/83/235; gE 1.75 base 164/42/169 | 46/160/167, glu 10/12/8 | 11/10/15, ach 193/194/191 | 191/179/193. Persistence by their own criterion over 6 seeds: gE 2.0 base 5/6, glu 5/6, ach 4/6; gE 1.75 base 3/6, glu 0/6, ach 5/6. So a glutamatergic GLNO (the T-bar majority call, 51 %) abolishes the bump at gE 1.75 in 6/6 seeds (vector strength 0.04-0.31, PEN 0.5-5.8 Hz, Delta7 10-20 Hz) and costs ~11 % of the bump rate at gE 2; a cholinergic GLNO holds the bump in 6/6 at gE 1.75, adds ~15 % at gE 2 (PEN 53-66 Hz vs 40-48, GLNO 140-174 Hz) and produces a spontaneous pre-pulse bump in 11 of 12 runs.
* Their 'persist' criterion (>= 8/11 inside and <= 3/35 outside above 22 Hz at 5 s) is brittle at the boundary and should not be quoted as a count without the rate / vs columns: in my seed-3,4,5 runs it returns 'no' for four runs that carry a clean 191-235 Hz bump with 10-11/11 inside cells and vs 0.76-0.78, solely because 4 (not <= 3) outside cells sit above 22 Hz (base gE2 s5, glu gE2 s5, ach gE2 s5, ach gE1.75 s5); and ach gE2 s4 is 'no' because the pre-pulse state was already saturated (pre in 81 / out 57 Hz) and the bump never moved to the driven block (centre 4.0).
* Protocol confound the doc does not mention: the pre-pulse state is seed-dependent to the point of changing what the pulse is being asked to do. Over the 48 runs I looked at, the 1 s settle ends anywhere from a quiet ring (base gE1.75 s0: in 14 / out 10 Hz, 0 cells above threshold) to a fully formed bump at the driven tile (base gE2 s5: in 198 Hz, 10/11 inside cells above threshold; glu gE2 s3: in 32 Hz, 7/11). The capture classifier handles this, but its denominators are 1-3 runs per condition, so 'captured 3/3' and 'captured 1/1' in the summary table carry almost no weight.
* Two structural facts worth adding to section 1, both verified: PEN -> GLNO is contralateral as well (GLNO_L reads only R-glomerulus PENs, 1,938 raw syn over 42 entries; GLNO_R only L, 1,558 over 42), so the loop PEN(side X) -> GLNO -> PEN(side X) closes within one PB side with 21 PEN partners at each end and no wedge structure; and signing GLNO also signs its 1,327 non-PEN output synapses, including GLNO -> GLNO 400 syn, which under 'ach' becomes recurrent self-excitation (0.275 x 60 x same_type_gain 0.1 = 1.65 mV per pair) and under 'glu' self-inhibition. Small, but it is part of what the glu / ach contrast measures.

## verify:score:slow  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* CLAIM 5 / docs/audits/slow_term.md section 3: '26,073 spiking cells (10.4 %) receive any [monoamine slow input]' -- the number and the percentage describe different populations and neither is 'spiking cells'. Recomputed from D:\Projects\flyverse\cache + flyverse/data/receptors_by_type.csv (scratchpad verify_percell.py, exact Brain pipeline: cap 60, gain classes, fan-in scale): 26,073 of 167,106 cells (15.60 %) receive any monoamine slow input, of which 17,973 are optic rate units; spiking non-photoreceptor cells with any input are 8,076 of 71,625 (11.28 %); the 0.10422564 stored in out/slow_term_stats.json is 8,100/77,716, i.e. all non-rate cells (incl. 24 photoreceptor targets). Every other per-cell number in the claim reproduces exactly.
* SUMMARY / slow_term.md section 6.2: "'gain' is the only mode that lowers the Kenyon cells as a Dop2R-led tone should" -- refuted by the task's own JSONs plus three new cluster runs. On the 1,1,1 weights the control is KC 1.68 Hz (out/slow_u_control_0.json) and gain 0.01 RAISES it to 3.05 (out/slow_u_gain_01.json); my interpolations give gain 0.005 -> 3.77 Hz / 1,656 active (out/out/v_gain_005.json) and gain 0.015 -> 0.94 / 1,552 (out/out/v_gain_015.json) against gain 0.02 -> 0.52 / 485. On the default gain classes threshold 0.01 (1.87 Hz) and 0.03 (1.88) are also below the control (5.58) while threshold 0.02 is above it (6.01), and additive 0.01/0.03 (3.80/4.27) are below it too. KC_hz is not monotone in the gain in any mode and the mode ordering is not separable from the run-to-run scatter.
* slow_term.md section 5b's mechanism for that KC drop ('the KC dopamine rows are mixed or Dop2R-led, so the KC tone is negative and scales their PN input down') -- contradicted by the task's own weights. Per-cell net monoamine slow load under the same --receptor-gain 1,1,1 (capped, fan-in scaled syn-eq) is POSITIVE for every large KC population: KCa'b'-m +93.58 (205 cells), KCg-m +1.51 (1,342), KCab-s +0.40 (657), KCab-m +0.39 (536), KCab-c +0.32 (488), KCg-d -0.66 (206), KCa'b'-ap1/ap2 -0.07/-0.15; only KCab-p is strongly negative (-112.43, 129 cells). In 'gain' mode a positive tone multiplies the fast input by >1, so the sign of the KC tone predicts the opposite of the measured drop. The large mushroom-body tones are DPM -188.91/cell and MBON11 +100.07/cell.
* SUMMARY: 'The Dop1R-only variant is indistinguishable from the DopEcR rule on the suite (no consistent direction; taste/bitter identical).' taste.MN9 and the two CALIBRATED bitter checks are bit-identical between each paired run, but the two Shiu bitter checks are not: additive 02 sugar 67.31 -> 95.61 Hz and sugar+bitter 5.09 -> 2.38; gain 02 118.84 -> 124.91 and 1.01 -> 2.91; threshold 02 69.38 -> 55.88 and 0.00 -> 0.01 (out/slow_{additive,gain,threshold}_02[_dop1r1].json). slow_term.md section 5a's stated reason ('the sugar -> MN9 path has no dopamine slow input, so those sections do not see the table change') therefore cannot be right, since the same section's Shiu setting does see it.
* brain.py LIFParams comment (line ~119) and slow_term.md section 2.2 on 'gain' mode: 'a +7 mV tone doubles the target's fast input, a -7 mV tone silences it (factor 0, never inverted)'. flyverse/brain.py:502-508 (_membrane_target) multiplies self.g, the NET synaptic input (signed sum of excitation and inhibition), by clamp(1 + g_slow/gap, 0, 4). For a net-inhibited target (g < 0) a negative tone scales the INHIBITION down, i.e. depolarises the cell toward v_rest; only for net-excited targets does a negative tone silence. The monoamine class is net-negative by synapse-equivalents (121,408 - vs 111,300 +), so over much of the graph 'gain' acts as disinhibition, not as a gain on excitation. Same issue in optic.py:_substep, where the factor multiplies syn = recurrent + photoreceptor + spiking input.
* CLAIM 9: 'rm_signgain walk_gf 68.4'. out/rm_signgain.json records walk_gf.p99_hz = 68.266 (68.3). rm_full (KC 37.34, loom 0.00, wind 4.62, walk_gf 6.22, rotation -1.73 FAIL) and rm_off_eager (20.13 / 36.54 / 43.67 / KC 2.90) reproduce exactly.

### Corrections
* Replace CLAIM 5's and slow_term.md section 3's '26,073 spiking cells (10.4 %) receive any monoamine slow input' with: '26,073 of 167,106 cells (15.6 %) receive any; 8,076 of the 71,625 spiking non-photoreceptor cells (11.3 %); 17,973 are optic rate units (the optic term); the 10.4 % in out/slow_term_stats.json is 8,100/77,716 non-rate cells'. Fix the key name fraction_spiking_cells_with_monoamine_slow_input accordingly.
* CLAIM 9 and slow_term.md section 5a: rm_signgain walk_gf.p99 is 68.27, not 68.4.
* CLAIM 8: the walk.power_max range over the four 26/1/2 runs is 70.4-82.3 Hz (u_additive_02 75.96, u_gain_01 82.25, u_gain_02 78.26, u_threshold_01 70.41); the 65.48 quoted belongs to u_additive_01, which is 25/2/2.
* Drop or qualify 'taste/bitter identical' for --dopamine-lead dop1r1: only taste.MN9 and the two calibrated-bitter checks are identical; the two Shiu bitter checks differ in all three pairs (up to 67.31 -> 95.61 Hz). Remove the explanation 'the sugar -> MN9 path has no dopamine slow input, so those sections do not see the table change'.
* Rewrite the 'gain' mode semantics in flyverse/brain.py (LIFParams comment), docs/audits/slow_term.md section 2.2 and optic.py's docstring: the factor multiplies the NET synaptic input, so a negative tone reduces inhibition on net-inhibited targets (depolarising them) and only silences net-excited ones. If 'silencing' is the intent, the factor has to be applied to the excitatory and inhibitory parts separately, which the present single accumulator self.g cannot do.
* Drop the claim that KC behaviour separates the modes; state instead that KC_hz spans 0.5-6.0 Hz with no monotone dependence on the gain in any mode (measured: gain 0.005 -> 3.77, 0.01 -> 3.05, 0.015 -> 0.94, 0.02 -> 0.52 against a control of 1.68 on the 1,1,1 weights), and that the KC populations' own monoamine tone is positive, so the observed drop at gain >= 0.015 is not the Dop2R story told in section 5b.
* State the isolation result: with the classical class at 0 the monoamine class can run at the round-1 scale 0.1 without the runaway (out/out/v_mono01_only.json: KC 7.88, loom_escape 66.79 PASS, rotation -3.49 PASS). The report's 'the redesign removes the runaway' should be attributed to the class split, not to the 0.1 -> 0.02 scale reduction; the sweep as run confounds the two.
* Quantify the scatter that the conclusion rests on, from three identical reruns of the adopted setting: walk_gf.p99 31.27 / 26.48 / 28.97 Hz (bound < 38), loom_escape GF 53.38 / 51.10 / 53.38, rotation -6.77 / -5.31 / -6.11. Everything outside the room simulation is bit-reproducible. So u_additive_01's 38.52 FAIL is inside one scatter width of a PASS, and the set of settings that reach 26/1/2 is wider than the four named (gain 0.005 and 0.015 also do).
* Ship the script that wrote out/slow_term_stats.json (e.g. scripts/slow_term_stats.py). It is not in the repo -- the same reproducibility gap the round-1 record raised as finding 6. Every number in the file is reproducible (I reproduced all of them from cache/ + flyverse/data/receptors_by_type.csv), but no shipped code regenerates it.
* Record the native-path defect seen while verifying: `scripts/benchmark.py --receptor-model full` WITHOUT `--eager` aborts every room-simulation section with `ValueError: cuda_sparse must be torch or warp; warp requires CUDA kernels` (flyverse/brain.py:378-379; self.cuda is forced False by _slow_on), leaving 9 checks MISSING while the summary still prints '18 pass, 1 fail' (out/out/v_gain_02_native.json / .txt, 7.5 min). Those lines are unchanged from HEAD, so this is pre-existing, not introduced by round 2 -- but it also means the round-2 'costs nothing when the scales are 0' property holds only on the Torch path: _slow_on is True for receptor_model='full' regardless of the scales, so the crash happens with --slow-gain-monoamine 0 too. Benchmark should fail loudly, or set cuda_sparse='torch' itself, when the model forces the Torch path.
* Minor: claim 7's 'runtime 16-24 min per job' is right for the 13-job batch (16.5-24.0); the 7-job batch ran 15.2-20.8 min. slow_term.md section 2.4's '20-24 min per job' understates the spread.

The disappearance of the runaway is therefore a slow-parameter effect, as the report claims, not an effect of the KaiR1D-fixed table or the TYPE_NT_OVERRIDE cache.
* NEW EVIDENCE, which half of the redesign removes the runaway (out/out/v_mono01_only.json; the report's sweep never isolated this): default gain classes, monoamine 0.1 (the round-1 scale) with the classical class at 0 gives KC 7.88 Hz / 409 active (vs 37.42 with classical 0.1), loom_escape GF 66.79 PASS (vs 0.02 FAIL), wind 44.07 (vs 30.71), rotation -3.49 PASS, tally 24/3/2. The class split, not the 0.1 -> 0.02 scale reduction, is what removes the runaway -- consistent with the classical class carrying 21,761,304 of the 21,994,012 slow syn-eq (98.9 %). (At monoamine 0.1 the Shiu sugar check fails at 42.39 Hz and walk.power_max is 87.3, so 0.1 is still too large for other reasons.)
* NEW EVIDENCE on the 'four settings pass everything the reference passes' result: the extra PASS over the u_control_0 reference is the Shiu sugar check, and it is a deterministic threshold flip, not a tuned property of those four settings. The Shiu value is bit-identical across my reruns (6.63 off, 129.62 at gain 0.02) and a monoamine gain four times smaller than the smallest swept still flips it: gain 0.005 -> 128.60 Hz, 0.015 -> 132.42, both 26/1/2 (out/out/v_gain_005.json, v_gain_015.json). slow_term.md section 5b already says this is 'a knife-edge, not evidence of a mechanism'; the KEY CLAIMS summary does not.

### Assessment

IS ANY MODE AT LEAST NEUTRAL? Yes: `--receptor-model sign --receptor-net-rule abs` on the adopted TYPE_NT_OVERRIDE cache. Evidence: 4 of 4 suite runs 27 PASS / 0 FAIL / 2 KNOWN GAP (out/rm2_abs.json, rm2_abs_r2, rm2_abs_r3, out/skeptic2/rm_abs_r4.json) against off 26/1/2 (rm2_off, rm2_off_r3) and 24/3/2 (rm2_off_r2, skeptic2/rm_off_r4; loom_escape 28.65 / 31.01 Hz under 33). Deterministic Brain-only sections: walk.power_max 73.18 FAIL -> 46.10 PASS, taste.MN9 5.85 -> 10.93, Shiu sugar 123.54 -> 137.42 (three Poisson seeds 137.4 / 131.1 / 133.7), calibrated sugar 4.57 -> 5.52 with sugar+bitter / bitter 0.0. Chaotic sections: loom_escape 57.2-73.7 Hz with 12/12 demo seeds escaping (off 28.7-41.7, 3/12), walk_gf p99 23.3-29.3 (< 38), rotation group -9.9 to -12.2; one measured cost: legacy loom.GF_peak 27.05-28.04 Hz vs off 36.7-43.9 (systematic -10 Hz, still PASS >= 20). The change is 210,935 |W| synapses (0.17 %; 127,462 glutamate flips, 83,473 histamine silencings); 0 entries of the object pathway (LC11, LC10a/b, LC16, LPLC2, LC4, T3), the optomotor readout (DNp04, LPT27/30) or the compass core (EPG / PEN / Delta7) change. Under the project's rule it is the right kind of change: the sign comes from absolute receptor expression, no gain is set, and the flips it makes are exactly the subset the round-1 skeptic said were defensible (class-rule Nmdar2-led tertile flips excluded). It is NOT yet adoptable as the default because (i) 26 of its 40 +1 rows are contradicted by another source (Tm9: Davis +1 vs Kurmangaliyev / FCA / Davie -1; L1: Davie -1; 24 ER ring rows: FCA class vs Davie class -1) and the builder has a 'contested' rule only for 'none' (build_receptor_table.py select_profile, lines 450-478) -- the loom gain is attributed to the Tm9 flip and no run holds Tm9 at -1 (there is no --receptor-table flag on benchmark.py / probe_loom.py although LIFParams.receptor_table exists, brain.py line 133); (ii) the cluster's shared cache still lacks the override (verified today); (iii) the default lives in LIFParams (receptor_model None, brain.py 130) and fly.py threads it, so adoption is a two-line default change plus 3 suite replicates, the object sweep and the pinned loom on the new default, plus a test; (iv) step 8 has to follow, not precede it. `class` (Shiu seed-0 storm 6.63 Hz at 847 spikes/step; Mi9 / L3 / Tm9 flips contradicted by every alternative source), `nonmda` (ON pathway inverted; walk_gf 51.6), classfb (21/6/2; Gad1 / VGlut class baselines call glutamate +1 under class) are not candidates. The slow term (`full`) is not adoptable as a default under the rule: its scale is a swept gain (0.01-0.03 x w_syn), its four 'passing' settings run on the class fast weights whose control fails Shiu (slow_u_control_0 25/2/2, 6.63 Hz) and the extra PASS is the knife-edge flipping back (gain 0.005 -> 128.6 Hz, 0.015 -> 132.4; the value is bit-reproducible, the flip is a threshold artefact), the runaway removal is due to the class split not the scale (v_mono01_only: KC 7.88, loom 66.79 PASS at monoamine 0.1), KC rates are non-monotone in the gain (3.77 / 3.05 / 0.94 / 0.52 Hz at 0.005 / 0.01 / 0.015 / 0.02 vs control 1.68), 'gain' mode acts on the net input, and it has never been run on the abs weights. It stays an experiment flag for assay 7.

IS HYPOTHESIS (a) CLOSED? Yes, as a receptor question, on three independent negatives: (1) profiles -- Tm5Y / TmY21 / TmY13 / LC11 / Y3 / Li19 / Tm32 carry 0 cells in Kurmangaliyev's type / subtype metadata (193,727 cells, 199 clusters; pupal 24-96 h APF, female) and no marker exists to key any of Ozel's 106 unannotated adult clusters to them (Nern SI 0 marker/gene occurrences, Davis supp. 1 0 drivers, Sup_Table_6 split-GAL4 fragments only); receptors_by_type.csv has 0 rows for all seven after the round-2 rebuild; (2) structure -- the lookup decides 0 input synapses of LC11 (342,941 |W|, 100 % fallback), Tm5Y 406,050, TmY21 201,184, TmY13 241,195, and changes 0 of the exact-tier inputs of LC10a / LC10b / LC16 / LPLC2 / LC4 / T3 under any rule; the only route is the medulla (Mi4 40 % of input under class, 3.7 % abs; Mi1 39 % nonmda); (3) function -- the NOTES-9 moving-ball protocol fails in 16 of 17 runs across off / class / abs / rectified with (ball - none) LC11 best-cell drive +0.03..+0.48 mV and LC10a +0.05..+0.16 mV against 7 mV, while the skeptic's positive control shows the chain works (a 43 deg object raises LPLC2 +3.67 mV, LC16 +1.20, LC10b +1.65 but LC11 only +0.21). Caveats to carry: the per-run FAIL/PASS is a coin flip at the 1 Hz criterion (1/17 PASS = 13 vs 12 spikes), only the magnitudes are informative; 2 seeds per mode; the drive-fluctuation noise floor (0.012-0.425 % of LC11 cell-frames > 7 mV with no stimulus) is untraced; the figure-ground Mi4 signal under class (z +1.7/+1.8) is real but does not propagate. The object item therefore leaves this plan and becomes a medulla -> lobula small-field wiring / dynamics question with probe_object_sweep.py and probe_figure_ground.py as its benchmarks; hypotheses (b) (abs: quantitative loom gain, still final-size coding) and (c) (no in every rule; DNp04 / LPT27 / LPT30 unprofiled) are also settled for the present sources.

OVERALL: round 2 fixed everything round 1 was told to fix in the tables and the source docs (every tables:* correction is applied and each source doc carries a corrections table), redesigned the slow term as asked, closed (a), produced the first suite-neutral receptor mode, and adopted one data-driven change (TYPE_NT_OVERRIDE). What it did not do: propagate the round-1 verify:score wording into the round-1 section of receptor_integration.md, write the index, touch step 8, finish the compass doc (the data existed 4 minutes after submission), or ship two of its generators. Its own reporting errors are all stale-number or overstatement errors (pre-override counts, 'deterministic 3 seeds', 'all 10 runs', '26,073 spiking cells'); no round-2 skeptic refuted a model-level conclusion except the KC-direction and 'gain'-semantics claims of the slow term and the run-to-run scatter behind several ratios. Compass: the receptor model is dynamically inert on the ring (sign-class bit-identical to base in 6/6 runs); the GLNO sign is decisive at gE 1.75 (glu 0/3 persist vs ach 3/3) and both EM predictions favour inhibition, which means the compass gains of cx_wedge.md were tuned against a silent GLNO -- a data-driven correction the project rule welcomes, but GLNO has no expression profile in any source, so the sign has to come from the EM predictions (MaleCNS T-bar 51 % Glu, FlyWire 3/4 GABA at conf 0.30-0.33), stated as such.

* KEY CLAIM 6's SEED ATTRIBUTION IS REFUTED: the pinned loom on the new table is NOT reproducible per seed, so 'new table seed 0 = 35 Hz with escape at 0.59 s, seed 1 = 28 Hz no escape' is two samples of a noisy quantity mislabelled as a seed effect, and the doc's explanation 'these are the first runs of this probe in which seeds 0 and 1 differ, so the seed-1 value is a real second sample' (docs/audits/receptor_integration.md R3.4) is wrong. My cluster batch sk3-rule-4a7a8b (6 jobs, 0 failed, 9.9 min, every job 'cuda ok NVIDIA B200', table md5 0381a446107e6050e75cc87b16d7f830 printed on the node, 48,295 entries changed) ran `python scripts/probe_loom.py --receptor-model sign --receptor-net-rule abs --seed 0` TWICE: out/sk3_loom_abs_s0.txt = DNp01 12 / 28 / 25 / 21, escape False and out/sk3_loom_abs_s0b.txt = 16 / 30 / 27 / 20, escape False -- the two traces diverge at the first loom step (spikes/step 5 vs 4 at t = 0.1 s) although their walking phases are byte-identical, and neither reproduces the task's seed-0 run (11 / 35 / 31 / 22, escape 0.59 s). out/sk3_loom_abs_s1.txt = 16 / 30 / 27 / 22 no escape; out/sk3_loom_abs_s2.txt = 11 / 35 / 30 / 22, escape at 0.59 s, GF 34. The conclusion (removing Tm9 / L1 / ER costs 2-9 Hz of the pinned peak and turns a certain escape into a marginal one) survives and is better supported by six runs; the '35 / 28 per seed' pairing and the implied per-seed determinism do not.

## verify:adopt  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* KEY CLAIM 10 / SUMMARY / DECISION, hops p-value "Mann-Whitney p 0.0003": that number is in no file. The generator (scratchpad/compare_r3_adopt.py line 182-183) calls scipy.stats.mannwhitneyu(a, b, alternative='two-sided') and prints '{p:.3f}', and out/r3_adopt_compare.log line 116 reads: 'hops default mean 1.500 vs off 0.188; Mann-Whitney U 226 p 0.000'. Recomputing from out/r3_sustain_default.json / out/r3_sustain_off.json (scipy 1.17.0): two-sided asymptotic p = 7.738e-05, exact p = 1.062e-04, use_continuity=False 7.105e-05 -- none is 3e-4. The effect is real and in fact stronger than quoted (hops 24 vs 3, U=226); only the printed p is wrong. path_m 'p 0.001' is right (0.001449).
* KEY CLAIM 3, 'glutamate 29,707 in every JSON' of batch r3-adopt-63e3de: only 4 of the 13 jobs write nt_counts. out/r3_default_{1,2,3}.json and out/r3_default_flagged.json record nt_counts.glutamate 29707 and cache_dir /mnt/beegfs/neurome/runs/r3-adopt-63e3de/cache; out/r3_obj_default_s{0,1}.json record cache_dir but no nt_counts; out/r3_sustain_{default,off}.json record neither. The override-cache identity is evidenced for the 4 benchmark jobs (and inherited by the rest via the shared run directory), not shown 'in every JSON'.
* KEY CLAIM 5 / A.2(ii), 'Demo loom escapes 12/12 seeds at 42.5-53.4 Hz': the escape count is right (12/12, and off 3/12 at 21.53-35.50), but 42.5-53.4 Hz is the range over the 9 no-flag seeds only. Over the 12 seeds the report counts (3 no-flag + flagged), sections.loom_escape.seeds[*].GF_loom_peak_hz spans 40.72-53.43 Hz (out/r3_default_flagged.json seed 1 = 40.72). Escape latencies 0.52-0.57 s are correct.
* A.1 bullet 'Not changed (not this task's files): ... probe_object_sweep.py ... keep --receptor-model default "off"' is stale in the current tree. scripts/probe_object_sweep.py (mtime 2026-09-12 00:03, i.e. after the 23:36 adopt batch) now has --receptor-model {default,off,sign} with default 'default', a patch_receptor that APPLIES 'off' explicitly, and writes config.receptor_model_flag. The adopt run's JSONs lack that key and record mode/receptor_model 'off', confirming they were produced by the older version -- so the run itself is fine, but the doc's description of the script is no longer true.

### Corrections
* Replace 'Mann-Whitney p 0.0003' (hops) everywhere with what the log prints and what recomputes: out/r3_adopt_compare.log line 116 'Mann-Whitney U 226 p 0.000'; scipy two-sided asymptotic p = 7.7e-05 (exact 1.1e-04). Quote 3 significant digits or '< 1e-4', not 0.0003.
* 'glutamate 29,707 in every JSON' -> 'in the four benchmark JSONs (out/r3_default_{1,2,3}.json, out/r3_default_flagged.json); the object-sweep and sustain JSONs record no nt_counts, only the shared run-directory cache path (object sweep) or nothing (sustain)'.
* 'Demo loom escapes 12/12 seeds at 42.5-53.4 Hz' -> 'escapes 12/12; GF_loom_peak 42.48-53.43 Hz over the nine no-flag seeds, 40.72-53.43 Hz over all twelve (the flagged run's seed 1 is 40.72)'.
* A.1's last bullet should be re-checked against the tree before the round is closed: scripts/probe_object_sweep.py was rewritten at 2026-09-12 00:03 (after this task's 23:36 batch) and now offers --receptor-model {default,off,sign} with default 'default', applies 'off' explicitly and records config.receptor_model_flag. ## verify:exp:retire  --  verdict: **mostly sound**

### Refuted
* KEY CLAIM 6's 'it takes discrete values (46.10 / 51.56 / 69.77 / 72.78 / 79.29)' (and the doc's 'The values it takes are discrete and almost run-independent') is refuted by my own rerun: no_al_ln_override gave walk.power_max_hz = 78.8925 in D:\Projects\flyverse\out\skep_retire\D\no_al_ln_override.json, a sixth value outside that set (its power_sustained 30.2848 is likewise new). The set is not closed; the honest statement is that the value is bit-stable for baseline / pair_gain_lpi_x1 / no_gf_damping and varies 72.78-79.29 for no_al_ln_override.
* KEY CLAIM 6's 'each ablation knocks the model back into the 60-80 Hz regime' (doc: 'each of the three ablations alone knocks the model back into the high regime') is false for pair_gain_lpi_x1: 51.5599 Hz (6/6 runs) is in neither the ~46 Hz band nor the 60-80 Hz band. Only no_gf_damping (69.77) and no_al_ln_override (72.78-79.29) land in the high regime.
* docs/audits/anti_runaway.md's 'compiles the connectome in-process in 14 s' for no_al_ln_override_connectome() is contradicted by the three shipped logs: out/retire_r3/no_al_ln_override_r{1,2,3}/no_al_ln_override.log say 'compiled in 69.4s', '39.6s', '30.3s'. No run took 14 s (my local recompiles are ~60-90 s).
* 'PN-named 42,005' (docs/audits/anti_runaway.md and out/r3_retire_structure2.json al_ln_onto_PN) is not reproducible from the shipped cache under any natural reading, and no generator script was shipped. Independent |W| of the 27 cells' output onto targets whose name contains 'PN': type 48,982 (2,779 edges); type ending in 'PN' 37,349; class containing PN 49,485; hemibrainType 49,048; flywireType 48,407; 'contains PN, excluding M_' 38,356. None equals 42,005. Every other number in the same paragraph (26,097 onto ORNs, 106,111 cb_intrinsic, 29,213 cb_sensory, 858 DNs) reproduces exactly.
* SUMMARY's 'a 2-job smoke batch passed' understates/mislabels what is on disk: out/retire_r3_smoke/ is a four-configuration smoke (baseline, pair_gain_lpi_x1, no_gf_damping, no_al_ln_override; 4 checks each, 0.5-1.8 min) plus out/retire_r3_smoke_hook/ (soft_cap_120, 1 check). It was 2 cluster jobs, not 2 runs; the wording should say so.

### Corrections
* KEY CLAIMS 7 and 8 quote baseline spreads that are pooled over 7 runs (the 3 retire baselines plus out/r3_default_{1,2,3}.json and r3_default_flagged.json), not over this batch: walk_gf.p99 baseline is 19.96-27.50 within retire_r3 and 16.40-27.50 pooled; loom_escape baseline is 47.40-59.46 within retire_r3 and 46.87-59.46 pooled. The doc states the pooling explicitly; the KEY CLAIMS do not, and should.
* My independent rerun extends one reported range: no_al_ln_override loom.GF_peak_hz = 32.2065 (out/skep_retire/C), below the reported 33.75-35.22; pooled over 5 runs it is 32.21-35.22. Still PASS (criterion >= 20); no verdict changes.
* The three 'replicates' per configuration are replicates of GPU nondeterminism only, not of seeds, for the check that decides the outcome: benchmark.sec_walk (scripts/benchmark.py lines 358-397) draws no RNG, and my job E with --seeds 4,5,6 returns walk.power_max / walk.GF_max / walk.power_sustained bit-identical to the --seeds 0,1,2 runs (46.0955 / 8.5189 / 21.3578 and 51.5599 / 12.2638 / 24.8167). The doc's table header '--seeds 0,1,2' invites the reader to think the replicates vary the seed; they do not for walk.*. Say 'replicates of the run, seed-independent in this section'.
* pair_gain_lpi_x1 is the thinnest of the three verdicts (51.56 against a bound of 50, 3.1 % margin) and the same document's section 2 records walk.power_max_hz as 'bistable 61 / 79' under the previous default. It now repeats bit-identically in 6/6 runs across two independent batches and two seed sets, so the verdict holds -- but the doc should note that this particular break rests on a 1.56 Hz margin on the check the doc itself previously called bistable, and that a re-measurement is the first thing to redo if the default changes again.
* Every retire_r3 JSON carries config.receptor_flags = {'receptor_model': 'off', 'receptor_net_rule': 'class'} (the CLI values) next to the authoritative config.lif = sign / abs. A reader grepping 'receptor_model' in the JSON gets the wrong answer first. Worse for the comparison set: out/r3_default_{1,2,3}.json (benchmark.py's own writer, line ~816) records `receptor: {'model': None}` although sign/abs was active -- a pre-existing benchmark.py defect, not this task's, but it should be named in the doc since the baseline's provenance rests on those files.
* scripts/retire_measures.py's unmodified report() prints spurious 'fixes' when a run directory contains no baseline (my jobs C and D: 'fixes: walk.GF_max_hz, walk.power_sustained_hz, loom.GF_peak_hz, loom.escape_cm, rotate.DNp20_flip_hz' with '0 baseline runs'). report_replicates() guards this correctly with `bool(base_reps)`; report() should get the same guard. Pre-existing and cosmetic, but it can mislead anyone who repeats the measurement one configuration at a time.
* Ship the generator of out/r3_retire_structure{,2}.json and out/r3_al_ln_override_stats.json as a script (the round-2 critic's follow-up 5 makes the same complaint about other files). Every number in them reproduces except al_ln_onto_PN, and that one is unverifiable precisely because the generator is not in the tree.

### Confirmed

My own batch (run dir /mnt/beegfs/neurome/runs/skep-retire-ffd29d, 5 jobs, 0 failed, 3.2 min, `--sections walk`, native, B200; results in D:\Projects\flyverse\out\skep_retire\{A,B,C,D,E}) reproduces every decisive number: baseline walk.power_max_hz 46.0955 PASS in 3/3 (pooled with their 7: 10/10); pair_gain_lpi_x1 51.5599 FAIL in 3/3 (pooled 6/6); no_gf_damping 69.7679 FAIL in 2/2 (pooled 5/5); no_al_ln_override FAIL in 2/2 at 79.2938 / 78.8925 (pooled 5/5, all > 50). walk.GF_max 8.5189 / 12.2638 / 9.2222 / 5.5060 and walk.power_sustained 21.3578 / 24.8167 / 36.91-37.49 / 30.28-30.37 all match. Every one of my runs records config.lif.receptor_model='sign', receptor_net_rule='abs'.
